451.29065pt}\|=1
, and likewise for
\top
and the Church numerals.

Then part (i) of Definition
4.15
is a
\Delta_{0}
statement, so it follows by applying Meta-Theorem
2.2
to Proposition
4.16
. Parts (ii) - (iv) then follow by Example
4.6
.
∎

In the following, for a set
A\subseteq\mathbb{N}
, we write
\chi_{A}
for the function
A\rightarrow 2
such that

\chi_{A}(x)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }x\in A\\
0&\text{if }x\not\in A\end{cases},

while if
\{\bot,\top\}
are part of the structure of a reflexive dcpo with numerals
D
, we write
\chi_{A}^{D}
for the function
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\{\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket\}
such that:

\chi_{A}^{D}(x)=\begin{cases}\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket&\text{if }x\in A\\
\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket&\text{if }x\not\in A\end{cases}.

Every dcpo is a
T_{0}
topological space when equipped with its Scott topology
[
14
, Remark II-1.4 (iii)]
. In
[
27
, Theorem 2.12]
it is proved that a
T_{0}
topological space
X
is injective for subspace embeddings in the category of
T_{0}
-spaces iff
X
is a continuous lattice equipped with the Scott topology. This has useful consequences for representations of the natural numbers in reflexive dcpos that are continuous lattices.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
(D,\mathbf{fun},\mathbf{lam},\bot,\top,(c_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}})
be a reflexive dcpo with numerals. Then:

(i)

For every computable function
f:\mathbb{N}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
, there exists a closed
\lambda
-term
M
such that for all
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
,
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash Mc_{n_{1}}\cdots c_{n_{m}}=c_{f(n_{1},\ldots,n_{m})}
, and therefore
\llbracket{Mc_{n_{1}}\cdots c_{n_{m}}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{c_{f(n_{1},\ldots,n_{m})}}\rrbracket
.

(ii)

\{\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket\}
and
\{\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are discrete in the Scott topology of
D
, and also are distinct elements,
i.e.

\llbracket{c_{m}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket
implies
m=n
.

If
D
is additionally a continuous lattice, then:

(iii)

For every set
A\subseteq\mathbb{N}
, there is a
d_{g}\in D
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
d_{g}\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi^{D}_{A}(n)
.

Proof.

(i)

By parts (iii) and (iv) of Definition
4.15
, and
[
4
, Proposition 6.4.3]

(c_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is an adequate numeral system
12
12

12

Strictly speaking, we must use
\lambda n.\mathbf{if}(\mathbf{0}^{?}n)\mathbf{T}\mathbf{F}
, because Barendregt requires
\mathbf{T}
and
\mathbf{F}
to be the Church Booleans, whereas we allow
\bot
and
\top
to be any representation of Booleans.

[
4
, Definition 6.4.1 and 6.4.2]
, which means that for each computable function there exists a closed
\lambda
-term
M
such that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash Mc_{n}=c_{f(n)}
. As
(D,\llbracket{{\mbox{-}}}\rrbracket)
is a
\lambda
-model
[
4
, Theorem 5.4.4]
, this implies
\llbracket{Mc_{n}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{c_{f(n)}}\rrbracket
.

(ii)

As Scott topologies are
T_{0}
, part (i) of Definition
4.15
implies that there is a Scott-open set
U\subseteq D
containing one of
\{\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket\}
but not the other. We start under the assumption that
\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket\in U
and
\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket\not\in U
. This implies that
\{\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket\}
is an open subset of the subspace
\{\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket\}
.

The map
\mathbf{fun}(\llbracket{\lnot}\rrbracket):D\rightarrow D
is Scott continuous, and
\mathbf{fun}(\llbracket{\lnot}\rrbracket)(\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket)=\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket
and vice-versa. So
\mathbf{fun}(\llbracket{\lnot}\rrbracket)^{-1}(U)
is an open set containing
\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket
, but not
\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket
, proving that
\{\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket\}
is an open subset of the subspace
\{\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket\}
. This proves that
\{\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket\}
is a discrete subspace of
D
, and the proof starting with
\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket\in U
is similar.

To prove the discreteness and distinctness of
\{\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, we will need the fact that there exist closed
\lambda
-terms
\mathbf{m}^{?}
such that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash\mathbf{m}^{?}c_{n}=\top
if
m=n
and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash\mathbf{m}^{?}c_{n}=\bot
otherwise. It is not difficult to prove directly that we can take
\mathbf{1}^{?}=\lambda m.\mathbf{if}(\mathbf{0}^{?}m)\bot(\mathbf{0}^{?}(\mathbf{pred}m))
, and
\mathbf{n}^{?}=\lambda m.\mathbf{n-1}^{?}(\mathbf{pred}m)
. It follows that
\mathbf{fun}(\llbracket{\mathbf{n}^{?}}\rrbracket)(\llbracket{c_{m}}\rrbracket)=\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket
if
n=m
and
\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket
otherwise.

Now, if
m\neq n
are elements of
\mathbb{N}
, we have
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash\mathbf{m}^{?}c_{m}=\top
, and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash\mathbf{m}^{?}c_{n}=\bot
, so
\mathbf{fun}(\llbracket{\mathbf{m}^{?}}\rrbracket)(\llbracket{c_{m}}\rrbracket)=\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket\neq\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket=\mathbf{fun}(\llbracket{\mathbf{m}^{?}}\rrbracket)(\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket)
. As
\mathbf{fun}(\llbracket{\mathbf{m}^{?}}\rrbracket
is a function, it follows that
\llbracket{c_{m}}\rrbracket\neq\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket
.

To prove the discreteness of
\{\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, we show that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the singleton
\{\llbracket{c_{m}}\rrbracket\}
is relatively open in
\{\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
. Let
U\subseteq D
be a Scott-open set such that
\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket\in U
, but
\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket\not\in U
. Then
V=\mathbf{fun}(\llbracket{\mathbf{m}^{?}}\rrbracket)^{-1}(U)
is a Scott-open set such that
\llbracket{m}\rrbracket\in V
but
\llbracket{n}\rrbracket\not\in V
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
n\neq m
.

(iii)

Given
A\subseteq\mathbb{N}
, define
g:\{\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightarrow D
by
g(\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket)=\chi^{D}_{A}(n)
. By the discreteness of
\{\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, proved in the previous part, this is continuous. Since
D
is a continuous lattice, and therefore injective
[
27
, Theorem 2.12]
,
g
extends to a Scott-continuous map
\overline{g}:D\rightarrow D
, and since
(D,\mathbf{lam},\mathbf{fun})
is a reflexive dcpo we can define
d_{g}=\mathbf{lam}\overline{g}
, and then for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

d_{g}\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket=\overline{g}(\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket)=\chi^{D}_{A}(n).

∎

We can formulate the theory of many-one degrees
[
29
, Definition 4.8 (i), (iii)]
in any continuous lattice with numerals.

Proposition 4.19
.

Let
D
be a reflexive continuous lattice with numerals. The following are equivalent for
S_{1},S_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{N}
:

(i)

S_{1}\leq_{m}S_{2}
,
i.e.
there exists a total computable function
f:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
such that
S_{1}=f^{-1}(S_{2})
.

(ii)

\chi_{S_{1}}\leq_{m}\chi_{S_{2}}
,
i.e.
there exists a total computable function
f:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
such that
\chi_{S_{1}}=\chi_{S_{2}}\circ f
.

(iii)

There exists a closed
\lambda
-term
M\in\Lambda(D,\mathrm{Var})
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash Mc_{n}=c_{m}
, and there exist
d_{S_{1}},d_{S_{2}}\in D
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi_{S_{1}}^{D}(n)
,
\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi_{S_{2}}^{D}(n)
and
\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}(Mc_{n})}\rrbracket=\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket
.

(iv)

There exists a closed
\lambda
-term
M\in\Lambda(D,\mathrm{Var})
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash Mc_{n}=c_{m}
, and for all
d_{S_{1}},d_{S_{2}}\in D
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi_{S_{1}}^{D}(n)
and
\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi_{S_{2}}^{D}(n)
, we have that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}(Mc_{n})}\rrbracket=\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

•

(i)
\Leftrightarrow
(ii):

Let
f:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
be an arbitrary total function. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
(\chi_{S_{2}}\circ f)(n)=1
iff
f(n)\in S_{2}
iff
n\in f^{-1}(S_{2})
iff
\chi_{f^{-1}(S_{2})}(n)=1
. So if (i) holds, and
f
is a total computable function such that
f^{-1}(S_{2})=S_{1}
, then we have (ii). Likewise, if (ii) holds, and
f
is a total computable function such that
\chi_{S_{1}}=\chi_{S_{2}}\circ f
, then
\chi_{S_{1}}=\chi_{f^{-1}(S_{2})}
so (i) holds.

•

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii):

Suppose that there exists a total computable
f:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
such that
\chi_{S_{1}}=\chi_{S_{2}}\circ f
. By Lemma
4.18
(i), there is a closed
\lambda
-term
M
such that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash Mc_{n}=c_{f(n)}
. By (iii) of the same lemma, there are
d_{S_{1}},d_{S_{2}}\in D
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
d_{S_{1}}\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket
if
\chi_{S_{1}}(n)=0
and
d_{S_{1}}\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket
if
\chi_{S_{1}}(n)=1
, and likewise for
d_{S_{2}}
. Then for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have
\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi^{D}_{S_{1}}(n)
and:

\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}(Mc_{n})}\rrbracket=\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}c_{f(n)}}\rrbracket=d_{S_{2}}\cdot\llbracket{c_{f(n)}}\rrbracket=\chi^{D}_{S_{2}}(f(n))=\chi^{D}_{S_{1}}(n).

•

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(iv):

Suppose that (iii) holds, and let
d_{S_{1}}^{\prime},d_{S_{2}}^{\prime}\in D
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}^{\prime}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi_{S_{1}}^{D}(n)
and
\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}^{\prime}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi_{S_{2}}^{D}(n)
. Then we have
\llbracket{d^{\prime}_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket
and likewise for
S_{2}
. Let us write
f(n)
for the
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\llbracket{Mc_{n}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{c_{m}}\rrbracket
. Then we have:

\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}^{\prime}(Mc_{n})}\rrbracket=d_{S_{2}}^{\prime}\cdot c_{f(n)}=d_{S_{2}}\cdot c_{f(n)}=\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}(Mc_{n})}\rrbracket=\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{d^{\prime}_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket,

using (iii) for the second-to-last step, and proving (iv).

•

(iv)
\Rightarrow
(ii):

Suppose that (iv) holds. The mapping
f:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
defined by
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash Mc_{n}=c_{f(n)}
is computable, and total by the assumption of (iv). By Lemma
4.18
(iii), there exist
d_{S_{1}},d_{S_{2}}\in D
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi_{S_{1}}^{D}(n)
and
\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\chi^{D}_{S_{2}}(n)
, so by (iv):

\chi^{D}_{S_{1}}(n)=\llbracket{d_{S_{1}}c_{n}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}(Mc_{n})}\rrbracket=\llbracket{d_{S_{2}}c_{f(n)}}\rrbracket=\chi^{D}_{S_{2}}(f(n)).

From this it follows that
\chi_{S_{2}}\circ f=\chi_{S_{1}}
, and we have proved (ii).

∎

Using random variables, we will be able in Theorem
5.9
to prove the existence of incomparable many-one degrees.

5
Random Variables

A
negligibility space

(X,\Sigma,\mathcal{N})
is a measurable space
(X,\Sigma)
equipped with a
\sigma
-ideal
\mathcal{N}
such that
\Sigma/\mathcal{N}
is a
complete
Boolean algebra, not just
\sigma
-complete. We introduce the notation
A(X)
for
\Sigma/\mathcal{N}
, as it is the
complete Boolean algebra associated to
X
or simply the
algebra associated to
X
.

For any measure space
(X,\Sigma,\mu)
, we can define the null ideal
\mathcal{N}(\mu)
to be the
\sigma
-ideal of sets
N\in\Sigma
with
\mu(N)=0
. Then
\Sigma/\mathcal{N}(\mu)
is a
\sigma
-complete Boolean algebra. If
\mu
is a probability measure, then
\Sigma/\mathcal{N}(\mu)
is in fact a complete Boolean algebra
[
13
, 322B,322C]
, so
(X,\Sigma,\mathcal{N}(\mu))
is a negligibility space. The complete Boolean algebra
A(X,\Sigma,\mathcal{N}(\mu))
is called the
measure algebra
of
(X,\Sigma,\mathcal{N}(\mu))
.

Let
Y
be a countable set. We first define, for each
y\in Y
,
B_{y}=\{S\subseteq Y\mid y\in S\}
, which form a subbasis of open sets for the positive topology (which equals the Scott topology) on
\mathcal{P}(Y)
. All notions of measurability for
\mathcal{P}(Y)
will be with respect to the Borel
\sigma
-algebra defined by this topology, and since this is a second countable topology,
(B_{y})_{y\in Y}
is also a countable generating set for this
\sigma
-algebra.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
X=(X,\Sigma_{X})
be a measurable space and
Y
a countable set. We define
\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
to be the set of measurable functions
X\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(Y)
, using the Borel
\sigma
-algebra of the Scott topology of
\mathcal{P}(Y)
. We define a
\Sigma_{X}
-valued order as follows:

\|a\leq b\|_{\mathcal{L}^{0}}=\{x\in X\mid a(x)\subseteq b(x)\}.

The corresponding notion of equality is

\|a=b\|_{\mathcal{L}^{0}}=\{x\in X\mid a(x)=b(x)\}.

If
X=(X,\Sigma_{X},\mathcal{N}_{X})
is a negligibility space, define
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
to be the set of measurable functions
\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
modulo the relation
a\sim b

a\sim b\text{ iff }X\setminus\|a=b\|_{\mathcal{L}^{0}}\in\mathcal{N}_{X},

which is the usual “almost everywhere” equivalence. Then
\|\mbox{$\leq$}\|_{L^{0}}
and
\|\mbox{$=$}\|_{L^{0}}
, defined by composing the corresponding notions for
\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
with
[{\mbox{-}}]:\Sigma\rightarrow A(X)
, are well-defined and make
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
into an
A(X)
-poset.

Proof.

We prove that
\|a\leq b\|_{\mathcal{L}^{0}}
is measurable as follows. First, define
\gamma\subseteq\mathcal{P}(Y)\times\mathcal{P}(Y)
to be the graph of the
\subseteq
relation,
i.e.

~~\gamma=\{(S,T)\in\mathcal{P}(Y)\times\mathcal{P}(Y)\mid S\subseteq T\}
.

\|a\leq b\|_{\mathcal{L}^{0}}=\langle a,b\rangle^{-1}(\gamma)
, so since
\langle a,b\rangle:X\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(Y)\times\mathcal{P}(Y)
is measurable, we only need to show that
\gamma
is measurable with respect to the product measurable space structure. We have

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\{(S,T)\in\mathcal{P}(Y)\times\mathcal{P}(Y)\mid\forall y\in Y.y\in S\Rightarrow y\in T\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{y\in Y}\{(S,T)\in\mathcal{P}(Y)\times\mathcal{P}(Y)\mid y\not\in S\text{ or }y\in T\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{y\in Y}\{(S,T)\in\mathcal{P}(Y)\times\mathcal{P}(Y)\mid y\not\in S\}\cup\{(S,T)\in\mathcal{P}(Y)\times\mathcal{P}(Y)\mid y\in T\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{y\in Y}((\mathcal{P}(Y)\setminus B_{y})\times\mathcal{P}(Y))\cup(\mathcal{P}(Y)\times B_{y}).

Measurability then follows from the countability of
Y
.

For the well-definedness of
\|\mbox{$\leq$}\|_{L^{0}}
, it is clear that if
a\sim a^{\prime}
and
b\sim b^{\prime}
, then
\|a\leq b\|_{\mathcal{L}^{0}}
and
\|a^{\prime}\leq b^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{L}^{0}}
can only differ where
a
differs from
a^{\prime}
or
b
differs from
b^{\prime}
, and the set of all such points is in
\mathcal{N}_{X}
.
It is easy to deduce the transitivity (in the sense of Definition
3.8
(i)) from transitivity of
\subseteq
, and part (ii) of the definition of an
A(X)
-poset follows because
\|a\leq a\|_{L^{0}}=1
for all
a\in L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
.
∎

We define
G_{X}:L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}(\check{Y})
as follows, with
a\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
and
y\in Y
:

G_{X}([a])(\check{y})=[a^{-1}(B_{y})]=[\{x\in X\mid y\in a(x)\}]

(5.2)

The first way of giving the definition shows clearly that
a^{-1}(B_{y})\in\Sigma
(by the measurability of
a
).

Proposition 5.3
.

For any negligibility space
(X,\Sigma,\mathcal{N})
and any countable set
Y
,
G_{X}
is a strict isomorphism of
A(X)
-posets
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}(\check{Y})
.

Proof.

We show that for all
a,b\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
,
\|G_{X}([a])\leq G_{X}([b])\|_{\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}(\check{Y})}=\|[a]\leq[b]\|_{L^{0}}
. This shows, in one go, that
G_{X}
is an
A(X)
-monotone function and also injective (using the fact that
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
is total,
i.e.

\varepsilon_{L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))}([a])=[X]
for all
a\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
).

We start by expanding the definitions:

\displaystyle\|G_{X}([a])\leq G_{X}([b])\|_{\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}(\check{Y})}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\|G_{X}([a])\subseteq G_{X}([b])\|

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{t\in\mathrm{dom}(G_{X}([a]))}\|t\in G_{X}([a])\|\Rightarrow\|t\in G_{X}([b])\|

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{y\in Y}G_{X}([a])(\check{y})\Rightarrow G_{X}([b])(\check{y})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{y\in Y}[\{x\in X\mid y\in a(x)\}]\Rightarrow[\{x\in X\mid y\in b(x)\}]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left[\bigcap_{y\in Y}\{x\in X\mid y\in a(x)\text{ implies }y\in b(x)\}\right]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle[\{x\in X\mid\forall y\in Y.y\in a(x)\text{ implies }y\in b(x)\}]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle[\{x\in X\mid a(x)\subseteq b(x)\}]=\|[a]\leq[b]\|_{L^{0}}.

Since
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
is a strict
A(X)
-setoid, and
\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}(\check{Y})
is total, implying that
G_{X}
is an injective function. So to prove that
G_{X}
is a strict isomorphism, we only need to show that
G_{X}
is surjective.

Let
b\in\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}(\check{Y})
. For each
y\in Y
, pick
S_{y}\in\Sigma
such that
[S_{y}]=b(\check{y})
. Define a function

a:X\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(Y)
by
a(x)=\{y\in Y\mid x\in S_{y}\}
. For each
y\in Y
, we have

\displaystyle a^{-1}(B_{y})

\displaystyle=\{x\in X\mid a(x)\in B_{y}\}=\{x\in X\mid y\in a(x)\}=\{x\in X\mid x\in S_{y}\}

\displaystyle=S_{y}\in\Sigma.

Since
(B_{y})_{y\in Y}
generates the Borel
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathcal{P}(Y)
, this implies that
a
is measurable. For all
y\in Y
, we then have

G_{X}([a])(\check{y})=[a^{-1}(B_{y})]=[S_{y}]=b(\check{y}),

and therefore
G_{X}([a])=b
, as required to prove
G_{X}
surjective.
∎

Since
\mathcal{P}(E)
is a reflexive dcpo and has a binary operation
\cdot
for application, for any measurable space
X
we can extend this pointwise to
\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
by defining for
a,b\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
and
x\in X
:

(a\cdot b)(x)=a(x)\cdot b(x).

For a negligibility space
X
we can then extend this to
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
by defining

[a]\cdot[b]=[a\cdot b].

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
(X,\Sigma,\mathcal{N})
be a negligibility space. Then the above definition of
\cdot:L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))\times L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))\rightarrow L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
is well-defined, and
G_{X}:L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}(\check{E})
is an “application homomorphism”,
i.e.
for all
[a],[b]\in L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
,

G_{X}([a]\cdot[b])=G_{X}([a])\cdot G_{X}([b]).

Proof.

In fact, we will prove the first part by deducing it from the second part. So let
a,b\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
. Then for all
q\in E
we have

\displaystyle(G_{X}([a])\cdot G_{X}([b]))(\check{q})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\|\exists K\in\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\check{E}).K\subseteq G_{X}([b])\text{ and }(K,\check{q})^{A(X)}\in G_{X}([a])\|

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\|\exists K\in\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-16.10072pt\hbox{$\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(E)$}\hskip-16.10072pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}.K\subseteq G_{X}([b])\text{ and }(K,\check{q})^{A(X)}\in G_{X}([a])\|\quad\text{(Proposition \ref{FiniteSetPreservationProp})}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\bigvee_{K\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(E)}\|\check{K}\subseteq G_{X}([b])\|\land\|\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-12.84894pt\hbox{$(K,q)$}\hskip-12.84894pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}\in G_{X}([a])\|

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\bigvee_{K\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(E)}G_{X}([a])(\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-12.84894pt\hbox{$(K,q)$}\hskip-12.84894pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt})\land\bigwedge_{k\in K}G_{X}([b])(\check{k})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\bigvee_{K\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(E)}[\{x\in X\mid(K,q)\in a(x)\}]\land\bigwedge_{k\in K}[\{x\in X\mid k\in b(x)\}]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\bigvee_{K\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(E)}[\{x\in X\mid(K,q)\in a(x)\}]\land[\{x\in X\mid K\subseteq b(x)\}]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle[\{x\in X\mid\exists K\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(E).K\subseteq b(x)\text{ and }(K,q)\in a(x)\}]\quad\text{($\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(E)$ countable)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle[\{x\in X\mid q\in a(x)\cdot b(x)\}]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle G_{X}([a\cdot b])(\check{q}),

so all together we have proved that
G_{X}([a])\cdot G_{X}([b])=G_{X}([a\cdot b])
. In passing, we have proved that for all
q\in E
,
(a\cdot b)^{-1}(B_{q})
is measurable, and therefore that
a\cdot b\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
.

If
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
such that
[a^{\prime}]=[a]
and
[b^{\prime}]=[b]
, then by Proposition
5.3
and the fact that
\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}(\check{E})
is a strict
A(X)
-setoid:

G_{X}([a\cdot b])=G_{X}([a])\cdot G_{X}([b])=G_{X}([a^{\prime}])\cdot G_{X}([b^{\prime}])=G_{X}([a^{\prime}\cdot b^{\prime}]),

and therefore
[a\cdot b]=[a^{\prime}\cdot b^{\prime}]
, so the definition
[a]\cdot[b]=[a\cdot b]
genuinely defines a function
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))\times L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))\rightarrow L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
. Putting this together with what we proved above gives us
G_{X}([a])\cdot G_{X}([b])=G_{X}([a]\cdot[b])
.
∎

We introduce the following notation. If
S\subseteq E
, define
K_{S}\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
to be the function taking the constant value
S
. This relates to
\check{{\mbox{-}}}
in the following way.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
Y
be an arbitrary countable set. For all
S\subseteq Y
we have

\|G_{X}([K_{S}])=\check{S}\|=1

Proof.

It suffices to show that for all
y\in Y
,
\|\check{y}\in G_{X}([K_{S}])\|=\|\check{y}\in\check{S}\|
. We have

\displaystyle\|\check{y}\in G_{X}([K_{S}])\|

\displaystyle=G_{X}([K_{S}])(\check{y})=[\{x\in X\mid y\in K_{S}(x)\}]=[\{x\in X\mid y\in S\}]

\displaystyle=\|\check{y}\in\check{S}\|.

∎

Recall that a subbase of clopens for the product topology of
2^{\omega}
is given by the sets
(C_{n,b})_{n\in\omega,b\in 2}
, where

C_{n,b}=\{f\in 2^{\omega}\mid f(n)=b\}.

These sets also generate the Borel
\sigma
-algebra of
2^{\omega}
.

In the following, we will also have to consider the (isomorphic) product space
2^{\omega}\times 2^{\omega}
, but for which we will need to describe the subsets in more detail. So for
i\in\{1,2\}
,
n\in\omega
and
b\in 2
we write

D_{i,n,b}=\{(f_{1},f_{2})\in 2^{\omega}\times 2^{\omega}\mid f_{i}(n)=b\}

Then for all
i\in\{1,2\}
,
n\in\omega
and
b\in 2
we have
D_{i,n,b}=\pi_{i}^{-1}(C_{n,b})
.

Let
X=2^{\omega}\times 2^{\omega}
equipped with its Borel
\sigma
-algebra as a measurable space. We define
\mu_{X}
to be the usual independent fair coin measure, which is to say, it is the unique measure such that for all finitely-supported partial functions
f_{1},f_{2}:\omega\rightarrow 2

\mu\left(\bigcap_{n\in\mathrm{dom}(f_{1})}D_{1,n,f_{1}(n)}\cap\bigcap_{n\in\mathrm{dom}(f_{2})}D_{2,n,f_{2}(n)}\right)=2^{-(|\mathrm{dom}(f_{1})|+|\mathrm{dom}(f_{2})|)}.

We take the null ideal of this measure as the negligible sets of
X
. Then
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
are random variables in
\mathcal{L}^{0}(X;2^{\omega})
. They define
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}(\check{\omega})
as follows, taking
i\in\{1,2\}
:

S_{i}(\check{n})=[\pi_{i}^{-1}(C_{n,1})]=[D_{i,n,1}].

(5.6)

We remind the reader at this point that
\|\check{n}\in S_{i}\|=S_{i}(\check{n})
because of the way Boolean-valued equality behaves for elements of
\check{\omega}
.

Lemma 5.7
.

For all
f:\omega\rightarrow\omega
, neither
S_{1}
nor
S_{2}
is the preimage of a finite
A(X)
-subset of
\check{\omega}
,
i.e.

\|\exists K\in\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\check{\omega}).\forall n\in\check{\omega}.n\in S_{1}\Leftrightarrow\check{f}(n)\in K\|=0
, and likewise for
S_{2}
.

Proof.

We only give the proof for
S_{1}
, as the proof for
S_{2}
is essentially the same. Using Proposition
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|\exists K\in\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\check{\omega}).\forall n\in\check{\omega}.n\in S_{1}\Leftrightarrow\check{f}(n)\in K\|

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\|\exists K\in\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-15.23384pt\hbox{$\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\omega)$}\hskip-15.23384pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}.\forall n\in\check{\omega}.n\in S_{1}\Leftrightarrow\check{f}(n)\in K\|

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\bigvee_{K\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\omega)}\bigwedge_{n\in\omega}\|\check{n}\in S_{1}\|\Leftrightarrow\|\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-9.8762pt\hbox{$f(n)$}\hskip-9.8762pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}\in\check{K}\|

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\bigvee_{K\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\omega)}\bigwedge_{n\in\omega}[D_{1,n,1}]\Leftrightarrow{\boldsymbol{[}f(n)\in K\boldsymbol{]}}_{A(X)}.

This being equal to
0
is equivalent to the statement that for all
K\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\omega)
:

\bigwedge_{n\in\omega}[D_{1,n,1}]\Leftrightarrow{\boldsymbol{[}f(n)\in K\boldsymbol{]}}_{A(X)}=0.

Then

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{n\in\omega}[D_{1,n,1}]\Leftrightarrow{\boldsymbol{[}f(n)\in K\boldsymbol{]}}_{A(X)}

\displaystyle=\bigwedge_{n\in\omega}[D_{1,n,{\boldsymbol{[}f(n)\in K\boldsymbol{]}}_{2}}]=\left[\bigcap_{n\in\omega}D_{1,n,{\boldsymbol{[}f(n)\in K\boldsymbol{]}}_{2}}\right]

Since it is an infinite intersection of distinct sets for distinct indices, the measure of the intersection above
\leq 2^{-n}
for all
n
, and so it has measure zero, so the above element has Boolean value
0
, as required.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

Then for all functions
f:\omega\rightarrow\omega
, we have
\|S_{1}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{2})\|=0
and

\|S_{2}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{1})\|=0
,
i.e.
these sets cannot be mapped to each other by any classical function.

Proof.

We only give the proof that
\|S_{1}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{2})\|=0
, as the proof of the other way is analogous. We start by ruling out some possibilities of what
f
is like. Assume for a contradiction that
\|S_{1}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{2})\|>0
. If the image of
f
is finite, then since
f^{-1}(S)=f^{-1}(S\cap\mathrm{im}(f))
classically, by Meta-Theorem
2.1
we have
\|S_{1}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{2})\|=\|S_{1}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{2}\cap\mathrm{im}(\check{f}))\|
and that
\|S_{2}\cap\mathrm{im}(\check{f})\in\mathcal{P}^{A(X)}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\check{\omega})\|=1
. But this contradicts Lemma
5.7
, so either
\|S_{1}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{2})\|=0
after all, or the image of
f
is infinite, so we reduce to the latter case.

If the image of
f
is infinite, then by the well-orderedness of
\omega
there exists an infinite set
I\subseteq\omega
such that
f|_{I}
is injective, and we fix such a set until the end of the proof.

We now convert
\|S_{1}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{2})\|
into Boolean-valued set theory.

\displaystyle\|S_{1}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{2})\|

\displaystyle=\|\forall n\in\check{\omega}.n\in S_{1}\Leftrightarrow\check{f}(n)\in S_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\bigwedge_{n\in\omega}\|\check{n}\in S_{1}\|\Leftrightarrow\|\check{f}(\check{n})\in S_{2}\|

We can then analyse what
\|\check{f}(\check{n})\in S_{2}\|
means as a formula in set theory:

\displaystyle\|\check{f}(\check{n})\in S_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\|\forall m\in\check{\omega}.(\check{n},m)^{A(X)}\in\check{f}\Rightarrow m\in S_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\bigwedge_{m\in\omega}\|\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-13.50235pt\hbox{$(n,m)$}\hskip-13.50235pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}\in\check{f}\|\Rightarrow\|m\in S_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\|\hbox{\hskip sebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}\in S_{2}\|.

So

\displaystyle\|S_{1}=\check{f}^{-1}(S_{2})\|

\displaystyle=\bigwedge_{n\in\omega}\|\check{n}\in S_{1}\|\Leftrightarrow\|\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-9.8762pt\hbox{$f(n)$}\hskip-9.8762pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}\in S_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\bigwedge_{n\in\omega}S_{1}(\check{n})\Leftrightarrow S_{2}(\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-9.8762pt\hbox{$f(n)$}\hskip-9.8762pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt})

\displaystyle\leq\bigwedge_{n\in I}S_{1}(\check{n})\Leftrightarrow S_{2}(\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-9.8762pt\hbox{$f(n)$}\hskip-9.8762pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt})

\displaystyle=\bigwedge_{n\in I}[D_{1,n,1}]\Leftrightarrow[D_{2,f(n),1}]

\displaystyle=\left[\bigcap_{n\in I}(D_{1,n,1}\cap D_{2,f(n),1})\cup(D_{1,n,0}\cap D_{2,f(n),0})\right].

We will show that this is equal to
0
by showing that the set inside the brackets has measure zero. Recall the finite distributive law, that if we have a finite set
K
and sequences of sets
(S_{n,0})_{n\in K}
and
(S_{n,1})_{n\in K}
then

\bigcap_{n\in K}S_{n,0}\cup S_{n,1}=\bigcup_{g:K\rightarrow 2}\bigcap_{n\in K}S_{n,g(n)}.

This is easily proved by induction on
|K|
. Note also that if for each
n\in K
, the sets
S_{n,0}
and
S_{n,1}
are disjoint, then the sets
\bigcap_{n\in K}S_{n,g(n)}
are pairwise disjoint as
g
ranges over
2^{K}
.

So for all
K\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(I)
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{X}\left(\bigcap_{n\in K}(D_{1,n,1}\cap D_{2,f(n),1})\cup(D_{1,n,0}\cap D_{2,f(n),0})\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\mu_{X}\left(\bigcup_{g:K\rightarrow 2}\bigcap_{n\in K}D_{1,n,g(n)}\cap D_{2,f(n),g(n)}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 2^{|K|}\mu_{X}\left(\bigcap_{n\in K}D_{1,n,g(n)}\cap D_{2,f(n),g(n)}\right)=2^{|K|}2^{-2|K|}=2^{-|K|},

where in the second-to-last step we have used the fact that
f|_{I}
is injective (and therefore
f|_{K}
is too). Since we can exhaust
I
by a countable increasing sequence of finite sets, the result follows.
∎

Theorem 5.9
.

There exist sets
T_{1},T_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{N}
that are many-one incomparable,
i.e.
we neither have
T_{1}\leq_{m}T_{2}
nor
T_{2}\leq_{m}T_{1}
.

Proof.

We start by doing Boolean-valued reasoning about
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
, as defined in (
5.6
), taking
A=A(2^{\omega\times\omega})
, and use the fact that the Engeler model
\mathcal{P}^{A}(\check{E})
is (
A
-valuedly) a reflexive continuous lattice with numerals (Corollary
4.17
) and the characterization of many-one reductions from Proposition
4.19
.

First, by the set theory meta-theorem applied to Lemma
4.18
(iii), there exist
d_{1},d_{2}\in\mathcal{P}^{A}(\check{E})
such that for all
n\in\omega
and
i\in\{1,2\}
we have
\|d_{i}\cdot\check{c_{n}}=\check{\top}\|=\|\check{n}\in S_{i}\|
and
\|d_{i}\cdot\check{c_{n}}=\check{\bot}\|=\lnot\|\check{n}\in S_{i}\|
.

Suppose
M\in\Lambda(\mathrm{Var})
such that for all
n\in\omega
, there exists
m\in\omega
such that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash Mc_{n}=c_{m}
. In particular, there exists some function
f:\omega\rightarrow\omega
such that for all
m\in\omega
,
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\vdash Mc_{n}=c_{f(n)}
.

Now we consider
\llbracket{d_{2}(\check{M}\check{c}_{n})}\rrbracket^{A}
and
\llbracket{d_{1}\check{c}_{n}}\rrbracket^{A}
. In the following, for ease of notation, we write an equality where the fact that an
A
-valued equality is
1
is meant:

\llbracket{d_{2}(\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-10.28127pt\hbox{$Mc_{n}$}\hskip-10.28127pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigvee\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbo Lemma
4.13

\displaystyle=\bigwedge_{n\in\omega}\|[a_{2}]\cdot[K_{\llbracket{Mc_{n}}\rrbracket}]=[a_{1}]\cdot[K_{\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket}]\|_{L^{0}}

Lemma
5.5

\displaystyle=\left[\bigcap_{n\in\omega}\{x\in X\mid a_{2}(x)\cdot K_{\llbracket{Mc_{n}}\rrbracket}(x)=a_{1}(x)\cdot K_{\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket}(x)\}\right]

\displaystyle=[\{x\in X\mid\forall n\in\omega.a_{2}(x)\cdot\llbracket{Mc_{n}}\rrbracket=a_{1}(x)\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket\}],

so the set inside the square brackets has measure zero.

Since (as long as
\mathrm{Var}
is countable)
\Lambda(\mathrm{Var})
is countable, the set of
M\in\Lambda(\mathrm{Var})
that map numerals to numerals is also countable. Therefore the set of
x\in X
such that for all
M\in\Lambda(\mathrm{Var})
mapping numerals to numerals we have
a_{2}(x)\cdot\llbracket{Mc_{n}}\rrbracket\neq a_{1}(x)\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket
has measure
1
.

For all
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have, by the definition of
d_{i}
, for all
n\in\omega
the statement
\|d_{i}\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket^{A}=\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket^{A}\|\lor\|d_{i}\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket^{A}=\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket^{A}\|=1
. By a similar argument to that used above, this means that the set of
x\in X
such that
a_{i}(x)\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket\in\{\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\bot}\rrbracket\}
has measure
1
, and by the countability of
\omega
the set where this is true for both
i\in\{1,2\}
and all
n\in\omega
is also of measure
1
.

Therefore the intersection of the sets defined in the previous two paragraphs has measure
1
, and so is non-empty and so there exists a point
x
in it. We can then define
T_{i}=\{n\in\omega\mid a_{i}(x)\cdot\llbracket{c_{n}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\top}\rrbracket\}
, and we have proved, by Proposition
4.19
, that
T_{1}\not\leq_{m}T_{2}
.

To get the final result, we re-run the argument swapping the roles of
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
, and take
x
to be in the intersection of the corresponding sets of measure
1
, so that
T_{2}\not\leq_{m}T_{1}
either.
∎

We finish by showing, by means of a counterexample, that we could not have done this by using the “fact” that
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(E))
is a continuous dcpo, because this is not true.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
X=(X,\Sigma,\mathcal{N})
be a negligibility space such that
A(X)
is not atomic, and let
Y
be a non-empty countable set. Then
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
is not a continuous dcpo.

Proof.

First, let
p=\bigvee\{a\in A(X)\mid a\text{ an atom}\}
. As
A(X)
is not atomic,
\lnot p\neq 0
, so there is a set
S\in\Sigma
such that
S\not\in\mathcal{N}
and
[S]=\lnot p
. By definition there are no atoms below
[S]
. Define

b(x)=\begin{cases}Y&\text{if }x\in S\\
\emptyset&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

where
x\in X
. This defines a measurable function
X\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(Y)
. As
S\not\in\mathcal{N}
,
[b]\neq[K_{\emptyset}]
. We show that
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
is not continuous by showing that
[b]
is not the supremum of elements way below it, which will follow from the fact that the only element of
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
that is way below
[b]
is
[K_{\emptyset}]
.

If the only element below
[b]
is
[K_{\emptyset}]
, then we are finished, so we reduce to the case that there is at least one
[a]\in L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
such that
[K_{\emptyset}]<[a]<[b]
. Define
T=a^{-1}(\mathcal{P}(Y)\setminus\{\emptyset\})
, which is in
\Sigma
because
a
is measurable and
\mathcal{P}(Y)\setminus\{\emptyset\}=\bigcup_{y\in Y}B_{y}
and is therefore a Borel set. We have
T\setminus S\in\mathcal{N}
, so we redefine
T
to be
T\cap S
if necessary to make
T\subseteq S
.

Since there are no atoms below
[S]
, there are none below
[T]
either, so
there exists a non-zero element
a_{1}\in A(X)
such that
a_{1}\leq[T]
and

a_{1}
has no atoms below it. We can repeat this argument to form a strictly descending sequence
(a_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
where for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
a_{i}
has no atoms below it and
a_{i}\leq[T]
. We can define
b_{i}=a_{i}\setminus\bigwedge_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}
, to get such a strictly decreasing sequence
(b_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, now with
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{\infty}b_{i}=0
. Since
A(X)=\Sigma/\mathcal{N}
, we can find a sequence
(V_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of elements of
\Sigma
such that for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
[V_{i}]=b_{i}
, and by adjusting negligible sets it can be arranged that
(V_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is also strictly descending. Defining
T_{i}=T\setminus U_{i}
, then
(T_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is strictly increasing with respect to
\subseteq
, maps to a strictly increasing sequence under
[{\mbox{-}}]
, and
T\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}T_{i}\in\mathcal{N}
. Define, for
each
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
c_{i}:X\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(Y)
as follows:

c_{i}(x)=\begin{cases}Y&\text{if }x\in T_{i}\cup S\setminus T\\
\emptyset&\text{if }x\in T\setminus T_{i}\cup X\setminus S,\end{cases}

where
x\in X
. Each
c_{i}
is measurable for the same reasons
that
b
is, and
([c_{i}])_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is a strictly increasing sequence in
L^{0}(X;\mathcal{P}(Y))
. Since for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
c_{i}(x)=\emptyset
for all
x\in T\setminus T_{i}
and
T\setminus T_{i}\not\in\mathcal{N}
, while
a(x)\neq\emptyset

for all
x\in T\supseteq T\setminus T_{i}
, we have
[a]\not\leq[c_{i}]
. But

\bigvee_{i=1}^{\infty}[c_{i}]=\left[\bigvee_{i=1}^{\infty}c_{i}\right]=[b]

because
T\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}T_{i}\in\mathcal{N}
. Therefore
a
is not way
below
b
.
∎

6
Conclusions

We have developed domain theory in a Boolean-valued universe of sets. Using the
measure algebra as the Boolean algebra we obtained a domain of random variables
which can be see to be a reflexive domain
in the internal language of the
Boolean-valued set theory
. We have focused on the pure
\lambda
-calculus
here but it should be straightforward to extend this to a
\lambda
-calculus
with probabilistic choice as an explicit primitive.
